Vane said on the 1st that the plan would be walked again on Tuesday the 4th. That second walk
is not on the page.

### The night of the 5th to the 6th — one pass

Times are as the page gives them or as they follow from it.

| Time | Event |
|---|---|
| about 11:00 | Kiva, at the gap in the low wall, feels the push under her palm "turn over": no longer going by, but standing still and deepening. She shouts to the roof. |
| **11:02** | The bell. Noa: "It's the drum, it isn't the mark." **A tear lying in the crown of Stopgate, between the tram rails, from the rail of drum house twenty-eight up to twenty-nine,** a hand wide, sixty paces and more. Eight knots rise through it together, abreast, eight paces apart, each on two legs, joined shoulder to shoulder by a white cord at chest height. Every one has a head, and every head turns. The planks over the gratings go white. |
| about 11:05 | Mara comes up the sluice ladder and leaves for the pump house. |
| 11:10 | Vane takes the hill as a response. Bottom forty houses down; top twenty-two up by the lane. Pru and Kiva at the gap; nobody strikes the post. Four fitters stand at the bar with the boy Ned still down the shaft raking the west groove. |
| | The Graywater van. A gate lit twelve paces below the lowest knot; all eight heads go past it. **Rhea Sorn cuts Eight from the ground on Pike's word** (sealed at 52, loud, red). A new knot rises at the top. She cuts Seven; another rises. Lowen Brask: "It's being paid out… That's a hold… There's nobody keeping it… It's the drain." Vane wires Mara for the bank tonight. Rhea holds her cut. |
| | Anwen (cutaway): Mace's line stands a walking porch along the east curb between any lit box and the string. At number fifty-eight the ninth head turns to a fanlight; she goes eight paces past the end of the wall, alone, and says so as she goes. Tibb and Stiles come up (Stiles a hand short, called by Tibb). Brask pulls the red handle; Perrin carries Mr. Garland out in his chair with his bird. |
| 11:30 | The blue reaches the stone sill of the black gate and stops there; inside the gate the main dives to eight feet. **The twelfth knot stands in the gateway.** Ansty draws the pin and puts the School's core down. |
| about 11:34 | **An arm, three-and-twenty paces long,** goes from the twelfth knot along the foot of the bottom wall, through the gap, round the lane side of the leaning post, and down beside the half-dragged lid into the sluice shaft. Perrin lifts Ned out by one arm first. The ten standing knots lean back together and ease: it is pumping the main. |
| 11:42 | Mara's slip: nineteen hands of twenty-four; pumps stopped two minutes on trial; **"The water is still moving… I cannot fill over this. Tell me when it is out."** |
| to about 11:55 | Nobody looks at Kiva. She says what it is, who has every piece that is not hers, and what she does if it is refused. Selka Dray arrives in an infirmary chair. Aske says the price. Rhea writes *Before.* Orla signs for the hand. Vane reads the direction. Anwen is fetched from the wall and reads the *why.* "I'm frightened." "Then yes." |
| | Four minutes to set. The step first: **four paces east is not clean (the main is under it). Kiva turns the mark north,** across the lane, and has it said back. She sees that the hem will then hang between the mark and the post, so whoever has the corner cannot be at the bar: Brask and Perrin to the bar, Wace and Moss to the mark. |
| about 11:57 | **"Hand, to Ember, to still. Rowan, changing on three, and on five."** On Dray's *in* the ward goes under the arm; on the haul it throws her a quarter round, north; fold; gap; bound; gap; **"Planted! Six!"** Ned shouts it to the lodge. The Works stops its pumps and quenches all twenty-four cells. |
| | At about nine she leans the corner to Wace's plank, with Moss at its other end, and shuts. Yarrow of Halden walks up and makes a third. She calls fives against Dray's fours for the best part of three hundred counts. |
| | Perrin and Brask (cutaway): eight turns; the ninth sticks on a hand of silt; then goes. **"Down!"** |
| | Mara (cutaway): the sump water rocks less, then lies flat; she sends *Still.* |
| about 12:02 | **"Back!"** on Dray's *in.* Three still-doors give the corner to the ground. **Ten blades cut ten knots** (Rhea Sorn the twelfth, in the gateway; Kellan the eleventh; Jude the tenth; Yeo the fourth). The hem lies down from the mark to the gate and down the hill. **Every window in the School and six hundred on the hill show a point of light once.** |
| | With nothing to hold it the seam goes on giving for a little: three husks (Jude, Gower, Twenty-Eight's reserve blade), then a string of four that stands on end and goes to the gate. Anwen calls the step in the middle of ten wards; Rhea Sorn stands behind its legs to cut it, with Nairn beside her. The closing crew works up the hill from the foot. |
| about 12:23 | The third pump starts. **The pumps stood twenty-six minutes.** |
| 12:25 | First slip: three pumps on three counts; the bank is out. |
| about 12:35 | Second slip, in Kellan's mother's hand: Mara's reading hand is starved to the wrist. |
| 12:40 | **The sealed medical reserve is opened by four** on the Low Lane's quench box. |
| **12:50** | **"Closed!"** (Bettan). |
| about 1:00 | The pump house: Aske eases Mara's hand; the keeper's book of 2604; Adlam's line. |

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Third Division, Crooked Line third team. **Seated; no longer provisional;
"Catcher."** Sponsor's line and every certificate condition unchanged; "not thereby
classified" (the Chair, the 14th).
- **On the Home Line's roll as catcher** from the 13th: not a seat, not a wage beyond a
  line-hand's day rate when called; called by a Line foreman, with her sponsor's leave while
  she has one, "to set a mark, to read ground, to call a count that is hers and name the ones
  that are not."
- **Three doors.** One more, in the field, on the 5th, under a Home Line direction and Rhea
  Sorn's hand, **offered and not asked.** Still uncertified, unclassified and "not to be asked
  of her." Three clean in all (a yard; the Pit; the Old Yard). Never twice inside a fortnight.
- **Injuries.** The left arm went candle-white to a hand's breadth above the elbow. Elbow back
  Friday the 7th toward evening; wrist Saturday night; palm, thumb and last two fingers Sunday
  morning. **Aske, Monday the 10th: "as before… and not a hair less."** The first two left
  fingers are unchanged: they bend, grip and report pressure and nothing else. **Nothing lit
  until the new year** ("not a step, not a ward for a tap"). The knee is still strapped and
  complained once, running.
- **The disk.** Cold on the 5th before, during and after; cold on the oak table in front of
  twelve people on the 13th. Fourth column entry of the 10th in section 4.
- **The dream.** No recurrence. No fever is mentioned on any night of this movement.
- What she now knows: a count that she takes through two hands is wrong by half a count; a
  planted corner can be leaned to another still-door as a plank; a ward curved the other way
  turns a throw a quarter round; the nearest road for a throw may be the one with the drain
  under it; the notch on her pen and the lamps' T-0 are two things.
- **Handoffs named** (the list of the 30th, as it stands at exit): the cord, all three turns
  (Pru); the slack of a hem on her left side (she takes a right-hand corner or is called to);
  tin lids with a lip; the pin of a seal and whether wax has gone off (Anwen has the wax); a
  button and a cuff; **the count of a thing that keeps time (somebody else's, named before).**
- **The pattern.** Answers withheld: at the Halden yard ("Sorn's right that it isn't hers to
  have offered"); at the leaning post on the 1st ("Tell me… Not mend it"); to the junior on
  the 16th. One taken and paid for: she knelt and called the slack herself on the 2nd, half a
  count late, and nipped Pru's fingers; she said so to the yard.
- **Offers.** Rhea Sorn's card (section 4), signed on the back by her four on the 15th.
- Her book has never had a page torn out. It has a spring, a summer and an autumn in it.

**Mara Rowan.** Reader of the trunk for Rusk; found that the drums fall in in the order the
Ferren main reaches them; put it under seal and was right. **Given the hill, the bank and the
stopping of it on the 3rd.** Found the sluice. Built the trough. Left the hill on the night
("I can only manage it from two miles off"). Took the Ferren bank out of step with
twenty-four hands, each cell on its own count, standing between each and the next with her
left palm; gave the odd cells to Rusk at the eighteenth. **Her left, reading hand was starved
to the wrist and was eased inside the hour with 1.2 drams of Team Seven's sealed ward-trace.**
A week before she reads a drum; a month before she reads a bank. Has written eleven sheets in
seven columns, *How the Ferren bank was taken out of step, with what was got wrong first,* for
the coast boat. Her sealed page is still sealed.

**Orla Dane.** Refused Vane's saw without a reason and said so. Climbed the Graywater stair to
tell Rhea Sorn that neither of them signs for the girl. Signed for the hand on the 5th: "I've
seen it clean twice. I've seen it missed three times… And I don't know that it will work."
Moved her crate a pace north. **Jessamy Hart has answered** (section 4). She did not write in
the proof; she gave it to Kiva.

**Anwen Pryce.** Has the wax. Had the sealed reserve out of the press on the 5th. Refused it as
salt on the 4th and proposed it as medicine on the 6th. Stood the uphill end of a walking
wall, went eight paces past it alone at number fifty-eight, came off a join before it had her
and said so as she went; **Mace has left her name where it was** (*Said so as she went*). Read
the *why.* Called the step in the middle of ten wards for the four-knot string. **Has told her
mother.**

**Perrin Cade.** Proposed the four-dram loan. **380 kilos for thirty counts, cut in the stone;
a place in his Hall's second team from the new year.** Carried a litter, an old man in a
chair, and a boy out of a shaft by one arm. Two to the bar with Brask. **Two ribs sprung, not
broken;** strapped again. Said that he does mind, all of it, into a bar.

**Kellan Renn.** **Has taken the Halden yard** (Tuesdays and Thursdays, nothing owed, his own
cuff; Birch: "They've not got worse"). On Seven's book "as a blade who's told where he's
wanted." Cut a clean rung in the yard on a catcher's word that was by the book and wrong, and
watched it cook a bank. Cut the eleventh knot on Pru's word. Read his mother's hand.

**Noa Bexley.** Sat the School's mast and called the mark and then the drum. Went to the
superseded stacks. Read the travelling mast on the 5th: two cliffs of 0.26 and a notch eleven
seconds after the second, **five minutes before the lamps jumped.** His page now reads *Not yet
weighed* and under it *Not one thing,* with the six-month mistake left in and ruled through.
Three columns (section 5); Adlam is second; the Chair has sent them to the Charter Board.

**Jude Talley.** Seven's Kill by name. Asked aloud whether the sealed reserve or the red bags
might be used; was refused; was thanked for asking. Cut the tenth knot and a husk. **7.8 drams
by name in one autumn.** Proposed a dram to Stopgate's wardens. Will stand at Saltmere if
asked by name, and was.

**Pru Finch.** A catcher with a sheet of her own. Folded a ward under a loaded cord twice
(nipped to the first joint the first time; twelve minutes). Had the gap on the night. Caught
the eleventh knot over a planked grating with a warden for a corner; sealed at 48; **wrote her
own red tag.** Told a Fire *not yet* twice. Rhea Sorn underlined the cord on her card. Asked
to the night van with Kiva and said yes "so quickly that I bit my tongue."

**Selka Dray.** In an infirmary chair, the leg in a board; on her feet "by the third month."
**Called the count on the 5th, on three terms** (a needle on Kiva; Marten carries the chair;
written that she objected twice and has not changed her mind). Her mast rule now goes to the
National Measure under seal. "I'd have seated you on that. I'd not have seated you on the
other." Not a friend. Means to beat Kiva in a lane at Saltmere.

**Lowen Brask.** Felt the post in the dark and said what wanted saying at the gap. Called the
slack for Pru. Named the string as a hold with nobody keeping it. Two to the bar.

**Rhea Sorn.** Refused to have the three-door offered on her card on the 2nd ("It's too
dear"). Ruled a sixth column, *Before,* at the left of her slate. Signed on the 5th after
hearing the price from the healer and the bad answer from Kiva. Cut Eight, Seven and the twelfth,
and stood to cut the last string. Wrote to the board; **the brackets came off Team Seven and
Old Four on the 14th.** Her card is section 4.

**Hollis Vane.** Foreman of the response. Gave the post three days. Found the lane sheet at two
in the morning. Third direction in the ledger, and the cost after. Moved, by his own account,
"a thing" in the watch; the River Flats made the motion.

**Ysra Crest.** Lent the hearing room. "Nobody in this yard will ask." A witness on the 5th.
Sealed and opened Mara's paper. Posted "seated." Sent the three sheets to the Charter Board.

**Others.** **Examiner Rusk:** wrong about the firebreak, said so in the street, took off his
cuff guards, handed over the trunk, pulled a handle and read the odd cells. **Mr. Adlam:**
astonished a sixth time; saw twenty-four dead needles lift at *back.* **Foreman Birch:** lent
his yard, his slate and his string. **Kellan's mother** (signs "A. Renn, grader"): the
step glass; found the cell that had fallen back in; knew a starved hand. **Pike:** the
handoff list; "Who called that?"; will have Kiva as his second. **Greta Stroud:** warned two
floors; took the end of the wall when Anwen was fetched. **Doran Mace:** the porch.
**Halloran:** twenty-four jars and no more. **Healer Aske:** two cards; would not round the
price down. **Wace, Hendry Moss, Yarrow:** the corner. **Wilf Marten:** the chair. **Nairn:**
walking, his stick thrown away, at Rhea Sorn's side for the last string. **Bettan:** closed it.

## 3. The go

**Three doors, the 5th of the tenth month, the Old Yard.** Price whole, as before: 0.02 (ward,
one tap) + 0.26 + 0.02 (bound) + 0.26 + 0.03 (stance) = **0.59**, from the team's line. Read
by Noa on the Circuit's travelling mast under the shed lamp: "Two cliffs. Twenty-six, and
twenty-six. Nothing between but the bound."

| Step | What the page shows |
|---|---|
| Step first | Disk cold. No white. Stance tried four paces east of the post: "a room underneath," eight feet down, bricked, keeping the count. **Not clean; no go there.** Tried four paces north of the lid, mid-lane: "only ground." A cross scraped with her heel. |
| The turn | "The mark's turned! It isn't east! East's the drain! I'm carrying it north." Said back by Brask and by Wace. |
| Catch | Standing on the half-dragged iron lid. Ward low at the right hip, bowl turned the other way (curve to the east, open side north), first two fingers clear. Under the arm on Dray's *in,* called a hair early as promised. |
| Carry | The haul of ten knots turns a quarter round in the ward and throws her north. Fold from the far edge to a penny. First gap: the work comes, the count does not. Left hand cold to the wrist. |
| Plant | Second gap "came out of a place that had been emptied in the Pit, and had only half filled"; she feels it pass the wrist and the elbow. Right foot on the cross. "Planted! Six!" |
| Hand off | At about nine: "Wace. The plank. Your end." She leans the corner from her heel to the plank and **shuts with nothing in the doorway.** |
| After | With nothing open she calls fives for three still-doors for the best part of three hundred counts, and calls *back* on a slack, one whole slack after *Still.* |

Changed conditions against the Pit: at night; on a longer arm and ten knots, not three posts;
the count called by a rival from a chair; the throw turned across the haul and not along it;
the corner given away at nine and not held for ten; the release called minutes later.

Aske's price, said before: "I think it goes to the elbow… I think so. I've no case to think it
from… It may be that she keeps a hand that grips and doesn't feel." After: "I've a case now.
One… I don't know that it would a fourth time; I think it took a deal more than it took in the
Pit, and returned it slower."

## 4. Records and rulings

**Mara's sealed paper** (sealed by the Chair on the 28th; opened in the street on the 3rd):
*If it is the drain: drum twenty-eight, on Monday the third of next month, with two dry drums
between. If it is the copper or the ash: nothing, and I am wrong. M. Rowan, Grade Two.*

**Mara's half sheet of the 2nd** (the assumption, written "so that it can be wrong in front of
everybody"): *That the water moves because the pumps move it. Stop the pumps and the main lies
still. Then a bank may be quenched and filled fresh, and it will keep the time that it is
given.* It was wrong within the hour.

**Aske's cards.** The 30th: single doors and dark hands from that morning; *A crossing, one, in
seven days, as before. The longer thing: not on my card. If somebody with a slate means to
direct it, he sends for me and I say the price to his face, before, with the candidate
listening.* The 10th of the tenth month: nothing lit until the new year.

**The *Standard Lane Manual,* eleventh edition** (withdrawn 2603; the superseded stacks): six
ruled columns in print and a seventh, *The lane with the crooked posts. Why: because the road
isn't straight,* with the junior archivist's pencil beside it. A plate shows two rows of posts
on a curve, the last of the south row a pace out with a bar across its top. The School "set
the posts fair" in 2390 (eleven days, in the bursar's accounts) and could not shift the last.
"It kept the why and lost the what."

**The Home Line's lane sheet** (a copy in the record hand of something older; from the chest
under the bearer's box; read by the Reader on the 3rd):
*The lane with the crooked posts. At Meridian, under the south wall, on the line of the hill
drain. / What: nine posts, set as the drain runs, which is not straight. The ninth is the
sluice post. It stands out from the wall for the bar. / How: run it dark, from the sheds. Call
the turn before the eighth, aloud, and say who has the bar. Two to the bar. Nine turns shuts
the hill from the low streets. / Went wrong: ran it straight and silent, and met the ninth.
Also: two came to the bar, and nobody had said which two. / Cost: a collarbone. A cellar in the
Hythe, the night that nobody had said. / Why: because the road isn't straight. S.V.*
Nobody on the page knows whose initials those are.

**The tenth edition's proof** (the 16th): the lane restored in seven columns. Kiva's pencil, in
the fifth column: *2680. The mark was called east, since east had served once before. East was
the drain. The step was tried first, and it said so.* In the sixth: *Called north, aloud,
before. Said back by two. Three others had the corner after six. Two had the bar. One had the
count, from a chair. K.R.* She did not touch the seventh and did not enter the cost.

**The Works' drain plan.** Its blue line across the School's bottom yard is "recopied from the
worn sheet, 2553," straight. The main in fact turns west inside the gate.

**Rhea Sorn's slate** now has six columns: *Before. Closed. Lives. Works. Tinned. Remarks.* The
entry of the 5th: *Before: the left hand, to the elbow. Her healer does not know that it
returns. Offered. Not asked.*

**Vane's ledger,** *Directions to the Unplaced,* fourth entry: *Direction to K. Rowan, catcher,
T7, attached, under R. Sorn's hand. Three doors in sequence, hand, Ember, still, declared.
Cost, as stated before by her healer: the left hand, to the elbow; he does not know that it
returns. Reason: an arm of a string of ten in the Stopgate sluice, driving the Ferren main, and
a bank that cannot be filled while it drives. No other hand on this hill takes it off the
water. Offered by the candidate. Not asked. The count is S. Dray's. The corner after six is L.
Brask's, or Wace's.* **After:** *the left arm, without feeling to a hand above the elbow. Four
days. Returned to where it was. The first two fingers as before.* And, where E.K. wrote the
other: ***Rowan, K. Entered on the Line's roll as catcher, autumn 2680. Hand as before.***

**The team book's *why,*** read by Anwen on the line: *Wednesday the 5th. Three-door. For: to
take an arm off the Ferren main, so that a gate can be shut and a bank filled. Likely cost:
her left hand, to the elbow, and perhaps kept.*

**The bearer's book,** fourth column, the 10th (witnessed by Vane): *Cold. Wednesday the 5th,
in the Old Yard, about midnight. After three doors in sequence, under direction, with an arm of
a string taken off a drain, and carried, and planted, and given to three others. Cold before,
during and after. / I did not ask it anything. It did not do anything. K.R.*

**The watch, the 13th.** On the River Flats' motion, seven hands flat: the roll entry above,
"for cause written. The cause is two entries in the Directions to the Unplaced, and what's in
the third column of each." Voted also that the bearer may lay the plate beside the School's
cast, in her own hand.

**The School's box.** A plaster cast in yellowed wool. Label under the lid: *Cast of the plate
of nine rings that was carried by the first teachers. Taken by leave of the bearer, for the
School's case, that students may know its look. The plate went out again with the Home Line
the same day, on its cord. It is not the School's and never was. We do not show the plate. We
show this.* The label has no date on the page. **The Reader:** the fifth ring is wider on both;
a flaw across the third ring at about four o'clock on both. "That is the plate of nine rings
that the first teachers carried. It was never preserved by anybody. It was *released.*" Noa had
drawn the rings from the fourth edition in the sixth month with the question *Is hers the
same?* The tenth edition will say so "out of the fourth. Word for word." Custody and history
only. The first book is still lost. What the plate is for: unknown; the Ferren leaves her
question in the book "with nothing under it."

**The keeper's book of the Ferren pump house, 2604** (found by Mara; read on the 6th): *The
11th of the third month. Keeper took ill in the evening; a fever, three nights. Entered here
because the needles did it too. / Dreamt that the roof was off the house and there was nothing
over it. Not dark. Nothing. And all four-and-twenty stood up together, and were looking. I do
not know at what. I woke and they were as usual. / The levee lamps went out that night and came
on again of themselves. No fault found. Not grid. I have put that in the fault column and this
in remarks, as I am told that a fever is not a fault.* Kiva sets it beside the one phrase she
is "allowed to have read," *the sky unroofed,* and "paying attention." Mara: "It doesn't tell
me what it is… I'd not build a shed on it… [It tells me] that it isn't you." Lumb: every pump
house on the coast keeps a remarks column.

**The Measure Hall board, the 14th.** *The Meridian Collegiate Circuit, 2680. The season is
read. / The Regional Measure: 1. Halden License. 2. Old Four. 3. Team Twenty-Eight. 4. T7.* (no
brackets). *The first company goes forward to the National Measure, at Saltmere, in the third
month, 2681. Entries under each team's own hand. / Entered by the Chair. Rowan, K., Third
Division, the Crooked Line: seated. No longer provisional. Catcher. Her sponsor's line and
every condition on her certificate stand as written. She is not thereby classified. I still
have no column. I have a page, and it has a second line on it. Y. Crest.* And, on Dray's
request, the mast condition "sent to Saltmere under seal," with both her statements entered.

**Rhea Sorn's card:** *Rowan, K. Catcher's second, to Pike. The night van, two nights in the
month, from the first of the new year, when her healer's card is up. Dark hands. No door on my
floor that is not on her certificate. Finch with her, for the cord, if Finch will. If T7 is
called on a night of mine, she is T7's and I go short. Until the last of the third month. Then
we look again, and either of us may say no. No seat is promised. R.S.* Kiva's two conditions:
the *before* column read out each night to all; her four have the first word. Signed on the
back by four on the 15th. It is to go back to Rhea on the first of the eleventh month.

**Jessamy Hart's card** (by the coast boat, the 14th): *Your letter was four lines. So is this.
/ I am a pump-fitter. Our harbour bank went into step on the 17th, at twenty past two. I knew
what it was before anybody told me. I knew it with the hand, the bad one. / Tell the one who
writes everything down that I should like to read it. / J.H.* He does not say that he forgives.
Kiva has not been shown it on the page; Orla puts it away when the juniors come.

## 5. The grid and the string (shown, not explained)

**What the page shows about the Step.**
1. The three Ferren pumps, on a bank in step, take their stroke together. Since the 17th of the
   ninth month the Ferren main, the storm drain for that side of the city, rises and falls on a
   count of four and four: two and a half fingers at the foot of Stopgate, a hair under the
   School's wall, and growing at the top of the hill.
2. The drums fell in in the order the main reaches them, two days apart: 19, 20, 21, then
   **25, (26, 27 dry), 28, 29.** Drums 22, 23 and 24 on the Kiln loop, where the copper leaves
   the drain, never fell in.
3. Two quenched drums did not stop it. Filled fresh, they were in step inside sixty counts.
4. In a lead-lined trough, a single plain cell on a stool, joined to a stepped bench bank by
   nothing but twelve paces of rising and falling water, leaned for an hour and went in.
5. **Nobody says that water can step a cell.** Mara: "the water in a box." Adlam's patent does
   not mention water. Why a tear's hold put the bank into step in the first place is not
   touched.

**The drill, the 2nd** (the Halden license yard; Birch's slate; a contained six-knot string):
1. The string ignored a lit gate twelve paces off, walked to the far end of the trough, and
   put two cords into the water. "It doesn't want the bright thing. It wants the thing that
   keeps time."
2. Kellan cut the top rung on Yarrow's word, with the cloth by the book hard against the
   trough. The open ash ran across the canvas and up the cord into the water. Yarrow bagged
   under thirty. **All six bench cells cooked together inside a breath;** the pump stopped.
3. **With the pump dead the water went on rising and falling. The string was pumping it.**
4. A fresh can on the stool went in at fifty-eight counts.
5. A boat-hook under the cord frosted from end to end and was taken; the fitter's left glove
   cracked.
6. Pru's ward, for one tap on the slack, folded before the haul, lifted the cord. Called half a
   count late by Kiva, it nipped Pru to the first joint. Called by Brask, it lifted clean, and
   **the water missed a beat and the cell on the stool fell out for a count.**
7. With the bung pulled and the trough empty, the cords hung ten counts and came apart into
   loose ash; the string read again and went to the gate.

**The string of the 5th.** Nobody on the page gives it a new name. The page calls it "a string
laid on its side" and "a string abreast"; Brask calls it a hold with nobody keeping it; Vane's
slate says "a string of twelve, abreast, every knot reading." It is never called a Barrel, and
no yield class is given.
1. The tear lies in the surface of the road over a shallow drain that is already keeping time,
   and runs on uphill toward ground that is not yet in step.
2. Knots rise through it side by side, eight paces apart, each on two legs, each reading,
   joined by a cord from which a frost hem hangs to the setts and stands above.
3. It does not turn to a lit gate. A head turns to a lit cell box in a house, and the cord
   bellies toward it.
4. Cut at the bottom end, the cord whips back to the next knot, and a new knot comes up at the
   top: "It's being paid out." Twelve rose; ten stood when they were cut.
5. Where the drain dives, the tear stops. It then reaches with an arm to the nearest open
   water.
6. With its arm in the main it hauls and eases the main; the water goes on moving with the
   pumps stopped.
7. Taken off the water and planted in dry ground, with the sluice shut behind it and the bank
   dead, it had nothing to drive. Cut on one word with the corner given to the ground, the hem
   lay down. The tear, no longer held, gave three husks and one ordinary string and was closed
   by the Home Line in under an hour.
8. Its ash is loud. What came up after the hem was down "reads as this year's ash reads, and no
   louder."

**How the bank was taken out of step** (the method the page shows): nothing in the water that
keeps time; the pumps stopped; every cell quenched on one word; fresh cans, each salted with a
pinch of plain ash that "has never heard a beat"; a card under each handle with its own count,
"not two alike in this room"; a reader standing between each cell and the next for a slow ten;
a cell that falls back in is quenched and filled again; each pump restarted on its own eight
cells. Twenty-six minutes. It needs the main to be still, which on the night needed the arm
off the water and the sluice down.

**Instrument evidence.**
- **The notch and the lamps came apart.** The notch on Kiva's pen fell eleven seconds after her
  second cliff, as on both earlier occasions. The lamps (every window in the School, six
  hundred on the hill, and twenty-four dead needles at the pump house, which lifted "by a hair"
  with nothing in a single can) jumped about five minutes later, at *back,* when the hem came
  down. At the Pit the two fell in the same instant. Noa: "One of them keeps time with you. The
  other keeps time with that… I don't know what either is." Whether other masts showed the
  notch on the 5th is not on the page.
- **Noa's three columns.** People: median Hold 6.1 against forty years at 5.3 to 5.5; three
  Fire readings at seventeen; Stonehand's plate 340 for thirty-one years, then 350, 355, 362,
  380 since the thaw. The other thing: tears on the district board, fourteen against a usual
  four or five; skins, none in sixty years and nine this year; a Flask that walked a bus; a hold
  stood by husks; a string abreast. Both rise, and faster since the fifth month. Saltmere
  (Yeo's wire): a harbour bank of thirty-six cells in step from twenty past two on the 17th of
  the ninth month and not out; tears on the coast board nineteen against seven; skins eleven;
  the Saltmere school's plate beaten four times. **A line Noa will not vouch for:** a ship's
  engineer "off a hull from over the water" says that his own port's bank did the same at the
  same hour. "Not entered as fact."
- Nobody on the page says why. "It's two columns." Sent to the Charter Board under the Chair's
  seal, Adlam second, with a line of Yeo's and Lumb's notion of asking who else has a keeper
  with a book.

## 6. Ledgers

**Team Seven's book.**

| Date | Entry | Sealed medical (W) | Held | Notes |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| Sun 25th, 9th (carried) | Held 5.62; 18.0 credited from the Pit | 2.0 | 5.62 | The 18.0 is entered in the next line. |
| **Wed 28th, 9th** | **Signed by four** | 2.0 | 14.12 | In hand 5.62 + 18.0 = 23.62. Call reserve, half a dram a fighter, five (Pryce, Cade, Renn, Rowan, Talley by name): 2.5. Second purse sheet and poles, "Finch to catch when Rowan is otherwise seated": 3.0. Lent at par to the casualty reserve for the boxes of the streets on dry drums, to be repaid: 4.0 (Perrin's figure). |
| Tue 4th, 10th | Loan repaid | 2.0 | 14.12 | Stated in the closing entry; entered with it. |
| **Wed 5th** | **Signed by four** | 2.0 | 13.53 | *Three-door, K. Rowan: 0.59, from held. One go. Only on a foreman's direction, under the officer's hand, with the price said first by her healer. Cade keeps the word. If not directed, returned to held, whole.* |
| **Thu 6th, 12:40 a.m.** | **Seal broken by four** | **0.8** | 13.53 | *For: the reading hand of M. Rowan, operator, Grade Two, the Municipal Works, starved in taking the Ferren bank out of step. On the word of a grader and by the hand of Healer Aske. Medical. Drawn: 1.2. Re-sealed: 0.8.* |
| **Sat 15th** | **Signed by four** | 0.8 | **25.53** | Call reserve spent 0.70 (Pryce 0.41, Cade 0.14, Talley 0.09, Renn 0.06, Rowan none); returned 1.80. Three-door line spent whole. Loan of 4.0 repaid. *Credited from Stopgate, by the foreman's pot: 9.0. To Talley, J., Kill, by name, a head's share: 1.8. To the book: 7.2.* Held 26.53. Then, on Jude's proposal: *Returned at par to the wardens of Stopgate, for the boxes on drum twenty-nine: 1.0.* **Held 25.53.** |

Check: 23.62 − 3.0 (sheet) − 0.70 (call drams spent) − 0.59 (three-door) + 7.2 (pot) = 26.53;
less 1.0 returned = **25.53.** The loan of 4.0 went out and came back. Sealed medical: 2.0 −
1.2 = **0.8,** and it cannot be made up: there is no plain ward-trace to be bought. Jude Talley
has 6.0 + 1.8 = **7.8** by name. Kiva's two step tests on the night came off her own weekly
half dram and are not on the team's book. Pru is paid off the Circuit's reserve card. No
warrant for Saltmere has been posted or entered; Kellan reckons ten for five heads.

**Refused on the page (the 4th):** the sealed reserve as salt for the bank ("It's medical");
the red-tagged bags as bait ("I wrote *Not for the ground*… I can't weigh what it would do";
Halloran: "Nobody burns the only one of anything while I've the key"). The red bags of the
ninth month are not touched in this movement, and the claim before the Compact's court is not
mentioned.

**The reference shelf.** Twenty-four jars of plain Handful ash written off by number, with the
reason, Mara signing under Halloran (one read out: *Sag Line. The 4th of the third month, 2511.
Plain. Held 106*). A further thirty for the drums refused: "A shelf that gives whenever it's
asked nicely isn't a shelf." The seven drums still in step are to be done with the wardens'
old winter stock.

**Vane's slate, read on the 6th at five:** *Stopgate, the night of the 5th. The bell at two
minutes past eleven. A tear lying in the road, held by the main under it. A string of twelve,
abreast, every knot reading. Closed, ten minutes to one, by the Home Line. / Lives: none lost.
Carried: sixty-two houses, fourteen by litter, and a school. Hurt: K. Rowan, catcher, the left
arm; see the direction. M. Rowan, operator, the reading hand, starved; eased inside the hour.
Cade, carry, two ribs sprung and not broken. Works: the School's core put down by its keeper
and raised again at noon. The Stopgate sluice shut, the first time in the Works' memory, and
opened at first light. The Ferren bank quenched and filled on four-and-twenty counts; it is
out. The pumps stood six-and-twenty minutes. Eight cellars in the Hythe, a hand of water. /
Drum twenty-nine, fouled. A plank split with the frost under the first knot, and the best part
of thirty drams went down. Its four streets are on boxes; the reserve says a month. Seven drums
below it are in step still, each alone on an open link. The Works will take them out one in a
day. / Tinned, clean: a hundred and ninety-two… Put by, red, on catchers' words and a comb: five
hundred and sixty and four-tenths. And a stripe of something that isn't in the yield book. /
Remarks: one. The one hole on the hill that could not be planked was left open, with a boy in
it, to clear a gate. The thing went to it. The reason for leaving it was good, and I'd give the
same order tomorrow. The remark is the event's.*

**The pot,** the Line's way, ground first: civic half 96 ("into this hill's boxes and the
Hythe's, tonight"); casualty reserve 24; schools 36; teams 36, **9 to each of the four fours
that stood it under their officers** (Halden License, Old Four, Twenty-Eight, Seven). The red
row is twelve bags, each under a catcher's name. Where the red row goes is not said on the
page.

**Not on any slate:** a can of civic stock in the Works' own store was cut with soot and would
not hold its tune at the eleventh cell. It is noticed and replaced; nobody takes it up.

## 7. New people and places

People (pronouns as written; author's choice):
- **Ansty** (she; keeper of the School's core house; short; a ring of keys; a red lever with a
  pin).
- **Lumb** (he; old; keeper of the Ferren pump house; a watch and a notched stick).
- **Ned** (he; fourteen; a Works fitter's boy; the rake; then the runner at the lodge door).
- **Officer Yeo** (she; a Saltmere company officer in green; brought the Saltmere house four to
  the Regional; cut the fourth knot; went south on the 8th).
- **Mr. Garland** of number fifty-eight, and his bird, **Admiral.**
- **Bettony** (she; a tall junior). The sandy junior with the plait is not named; she is about
  thirteen and was Kindled in the spring.
- Kellan's mother signs **"A. Renn, grader."** She is otherwise still unnamed.
- Unnamed: the School's bursar (he); the fitter in the leather apron who came with a saw; the
  Works apprentice on the bicycle; the warden of drum house twenty-five and the eleven wardens
  of Stopgate; a School porter; the Works clerk at the sounder; six of the ten blades and most
  of the catchers on the hill (the slate says only that the Halden four cut two knots and
  caught one); Twenty-Eight's ward and reserve blade,
  still.
- Returning by name: Hendry Moss of Stonehand (chapter 15), Bettan, Nairn, Tibb, Stiles, Doran
  Mace, Ruddock, Greta Stroud, Halloran, the Reader, Mrs. Pryce (seen, floury, not speaking).

Places and things:
- **Stopgate:** the long straight hill from the Brickfields road to the School's bottom wall, a
  tram line up the middle, sixty-two houses. Drum houses twenty-five (the foot) to twenty-nine
  (near the top; by the page's knot spacing about thirty-two paces below the gate, which is
  not stated); a grating between the rails every twenty paces. Nobody had
  asked why it was called that.
- **The Kiln loop:** where the copper leaves the road after drum twenty-one and goes east round
  the old brick kilns (drums 22–24).
- **A drum house:** a brick hut with a green door, one drum, a needle under glass.
- **The School's bottom:** the bottom wall (thirty feet of brick) and the black oak cart gate
  with a wicket and the School's device, "a line with a kink in it"; the bottom yard, thirty
  paces square; the core house on the east with the Low Lane against its south wall and the
  New Lane behind; a low wall with a gap on the west; the Old Yard beyond the gap, forty paces
  to the sheds, two rows of posts, **the leaning post eight paces in, a yard and a half from
  the wall.**
- **The sluice:** an iron lid where Kiva's cross was; a square brick shaft eight feet deep; an
  iron penstock in two grooves a foot above the water; a screw on the rake coming up inside the
  post; a square socket in the "split"; an ash bar, now on two hooks on the wall. A second,
  small mouth from the core house enters above the gate. Nine turns shuts the hill from the low
  streets; shut, the hill's rain has nowhere to go.
- **The Ferren main:** the storm drain; shallow under Stopgate, eight feet down inside the
  gate; it comes in under the gate, runs west along the wall to the post, and goes on west
  under the south row.
- The Ferren pump house's sump hatch and sounder; the keeper's books in calf; the reference
  shelf's plain row; **the municipal wire** with a key in the keeper's lodge; the Charter
  Board; **the National Measure, at Saltmere.**

Names checked by search against every book's manuscript and registry in this repository and
`universe/` before use: Stopgate, Lumb, Ansty, Yeo, Garland, Admiral, Bettony, Ned, the Kiln
loop. One first choice was dropped: "Tam" (too near Tamar Vey of the universe bible) became
Ned. "Ostrey" was considered for a third Graywater blade and not used, since chapter 30 has it
as a name out of the past.

## 8. Plants and open threads

| Plant | Status at exit |
|---|---|
| Three doors open; no column | **Seated, unclassified.** A second line on the Chair's *Witnessed* page. No rank, Hold, Draw or bench figure is re-measured. |
| The crooked post | **Paid.** It is the handle of a sluice gate, "aimed," on a lane laid over a drain that turns. The manual kept the why; the Line kept the what. |
| A meter and a shutdown | The meter was a cork. The shutdown was under Kiva's cross. |
| The disk | Recognized as the plate of nine rings of the first teachers, released by appointment. Cold. **What it is for: unknown. The first book: lost.** |
| The dream | A second, independent line (a pump keeper, 2604) has the roof off, nothing over it, and every needle "looking," on a night in the fault column. No cause, date for Annick, meaning or use. **Not named on the page as the Unroofed Sky.** |
| The notch and T-0 | **Two things.** One follows Kiva's cliff by eleven seconds. One follows the fall of a hold. Neither is explained. |
| Human and spawn escalation | Two columns, and a third from Saltmere. Correlation and a quickening since the fifth month. No cause. |
| Two-finger sensation | As before. Permanent. |
| The Ferren bank | **Out of step.** |
| The Brickfields trunk | Seven drums (19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, 28) still in step, each alone on an open link; one a day. **Drum twenty-nine fouled;** four streets on boxes about a month. The School's core house never fell in. |
| Saltmere | A harbour bank of thirty-six in step since the same minute and not out. "Nobody at Saltmere… knows how to take a bank out." Yeo will ask for the Works' reader again in the spring. |
| The Charter Board | Three sheets sent. No answer on the page. |
| The National Measure | Saltmere, the third month, 2681. The first company goes: Halden License, Old Four, Twenty-Eight, Seven. Dray's mast rule goes with it. |
| Rhea Sorn | A bounded card, to the last of the third month. |
| Jessamy Hart | Answered. Wants to read what Kiva has written down. Kiva does not yet know. |
| Selka Dray | Unpersuaded; means to beat Kiva at Saltmere. |
| Team Seven | Fourth; second step in its own right; held 25.53; sealed 0.8. |

| The Order of the Even Hand | **Not on the page in this movement.** Its door is still "on the latch." |
| Mara's sealed page; the 2446 entry; the first bearer's book | Untouched. |
| A cut can in the Works' store | Noticed. Open. |

## 9. Items for owner or editorial attention

Routes the author chose where the packet left room, and places where the draft may have gone
further than intended:

1. **The mechanism is a storm drain.** The Step walks the Ferren main because three stepped
   pumps make the water rise and fall. The page shows order, a sealed prediction, a field
   confirmation and a bench confirmation; it never says that water can step a cell. This turns
   Movement Five's "nine drums" into five and its eighteen days into ten (from the 27th).
2. **The first isolation plan is a firebreak,** and it fails without making anything worse. Its
   cost is six hundred households on boxes for five days and an examiner's public admission.
3. **The bad assumption in the drill** is "stop the pumps and the main lies still." A string
   with an arm in the water is itself a pump.
4. **The new expression is not named and not classed.** "A string laid on its side"; twelve
   rose and ten stood; 720 drams by the book of a knot, a Flask and a half. It is never called
   a Barrel, and the registry's "Breach" is not used. If the owner wants a name or a yield
   class for it, the foreman's slate or Halloran's shelf is the place.
5. **The crooked post is a working shut-off.** This is the largest invention of the movement.
   It rests on the prologue's plural ("the lane with the crooked posts") and on S.V.'s line,
   which stands unaltered. New on the page: the School straightened the lane in 2390; the
   Line's sheet; the penstock; "Stopgate." Review should check chapters 3, 8, 17 and 36 for
   anything that makes a spindle inside the post impossible (it is "black, split, two iron
   bands, leans left," and Kiva has laid a hand on it).
6. **Kiva's cross was over the sluice lid.** Eight feet of ground and a brick barrel are the
   page's reason that six clean stances never rang. Review should weigh it.
7. **What the three doors did** is again a catch, turned: the throw taken across the haul, the
   corner planted in ground chosen by a step test, **given to three still-doors at about nine,**
   and released minutes later on Kiva's word. She holds it for about four counts.
8. **The cost was the arm for four days, returned.** The packet forbade restoring the fingers
   and required the hand to close "as before." The page states the risk first and has Aske
   refuse to say why it came back.
9. **The sealed reserve was opened for Mara's reading hand,** on Anwen's proposal, with Kiva
   signing fourth. Mara's injury is new and heals; it costs her a month of bank work.
10. **The grid answer is local and provisional:** one bank out, seven drums to do, one drum
    fouled, Saltmere's bank still in.
11. **Kiva's public place is double:** the Home Line's roll (the Tansy Wold line of 2471,
    repeated) and the Chair's "seated. No longer provisional." The brackets come off Seven and
    Old Four. No one on the page says that the call counted as the owed Flask except Pike.
12. **Rhea Sorn's offer** is two nights a month as Pike's second, for three months. Kiva
    conditions it and takes it to her four, who sign.
13. **The disk's recognition** runs through a plaster cast and a label, with the Reader as the
    credible witness and Noa's drawing as the earlier notice. The label is undated. It asserts
    "the first teachers" and nothing earlier.
14. **The historical echo is a pump keeper of 2604,** not a Glory master. The universe bible
    says that "a small number of high-rank practitioners" record the fragments. The owner may
    prefer a higher-rank witness; the keeper was chosen because the 2604 T-0 entry already
    stood in the Works' register and because it makes the phenomenon wider as well as older.
15. **Saltmere's bank fell in the same minute,** and an unvouched line carries it overseas.
    This is the plant for Books Two and Three and should be checked against the series map.
16. **Jessamy Hart answers.** Four lines; a pump-fitter; "the hand, the bad one."
17. **Kellan takes the Halden yard** and stays on Seven's book. **Perrin's 380** is a new
    figure. **Rhea's sixth column** and **Selka Dray as count-caller** are the author's.
18. **Kellan's mother is given an initial** ("A. Renn"). **"A. Renn," Ansty, Lumb, Yeo, Ned,
    Garland and Bettony** are pending owner review, as are the pronouns in section 7.
19. **Movement length is 50,866 words** (`wc -w`). Chapters run 5,147 to 7,250. Forty-four
    sections, about 1,156 words a section. **Secondary viewpoint is 12.8%** by plain section
    word count (Mara 1,730; Anwen 1,294; Rhea 1,131; Kellan 1,035; Perrin 863; Orla 439; all
    established lenses); Kiva holds 87.2%. These are counts, not craft measurements. The
    formula's targets were written toward and have not been measured.
20. Small facts taken from the checkpoints and not re-read in the manuscript should be checked:
    the layout of the Old Yard and the bottom yard against chapters 3, 9 and 19; that the
    School's core house has an iron door and no window; that Rhea Sorn was pulled from a culvert
    in 2664; Stonehand's plate figures; Hendry Moss; the weir meadow's iron plate; the Reader's
    thirty-one years; that the fourth edition of the *Founders' History* has a plate of the
    rings; the district table's tear count of fourteen. Two
    small ambiguities are on the page: Vane says on the 10th that he "moved a thing" when the
    watch sat on the 9th, and on the 13th the River Flats makes the roll motion and the seven
    vote in Kiva's presence; and that motion cites "two entries" in the Directions to the
    Unplaced, where Kiva has three directions (the two with a cost column are meant).
21. Arithmetic worth a mechanical check: the drain dates (25 on the 27th; 28 on the 3rd; 29 on
    the 5th; the core house due the 7th); "ten" days; the knot numbering (twelve named, Eight
    and Seven cut early, ten standing; positions at eight paces from 29's rail to the gate);
    the red figure 560.4; the pot (96, 24, 36, 36, 9); the team book; "eleven weeks and four
    days" to the new year; seventy-seven years since 2603; the pumps' twenty-six minutes
    against Lumb's twenty-five.
22. **Chapters were committed by another process as each landed.** Small continuity corrections
    and two additions were made in the working tree afterward (see `AUTHORSHIP.md`). The
    working tree is the first draft.

## 10. Deliberately not done

The disk does not warm, teach, speak, map, choose, open a door or answer; it is recognized and
it is cold. The dream does not recur; the name Unroofed Sky is not on the page; nobody
identifies the figures, the filter, the Makers, the cause of the Fall or the disk's origin. The
keeper's line and the Annick phrase are set side by side as testimony and nobody builds on
them. Nobody says why a drain steps a cell, why a tear throws stepped ash, why husks stood a
hold, or what the notch or the lamps are. The three-door configuration is done once, under a
written direction and an officer's hand, offered and not asked, with the price said first by
her healer; it is not certified, ranked, classified, taught or repeated, and no rank or bench
figure is re-measured. The fingers are not cured by ash, rank, rest or the result. The sealed
reserve is opened only as medicine and entered as such; the red bags are not used; no free
practice ash appears. Students close no tear, cut no knot except under a foreman's slate, and
touch no municipal cell except the Bench-passed students at the pump house under the Works'
operators. The Home Line closes the tear. The Municipal Works and the Halden Works stay
distinct, and neither is a villain; nor is the School. Selka Dray is not made a friend. No
secondary viewpoint is new. The offer is bounded and conditioned. Book Two is not started; its
invitations are a notice, a card, a wire and three sheets. No commit, push, publication,
narration, review, scoring or repair was done by the author.

## 11. What Book Two opens on

State on the evening of Sunday the 16th of the tenth month, 2680:

- **The grid.** Meridian has taken one municipal bank out of step by a method that is written
  down in seven columns and on its way to the coast. Seven drums wait their turn. Saltmere's
  harbour bank is still one engine. A ship's engineer says that it is not only this country.
- **The rifts.** A tear can lie down in a road over water that keeps time, pay out a string
  abreast, and pump. A long-forgotten sluice stopped one. Nobody knows how many
  drains have a gate.
- **The record.** Three sheets are before the Charter Board. A pump keeper of 2604 and a Master
  of the 2440s have both written that there was no roof. Every pump house on the coast has a
  remarks column that nobody has read. A plate has been recognized and has said nothing.
- **The people.** Kiva is seated, unclassified, on the Line's roll, stood down from every door
  until the new year, with a company's card to the end of the third month and eight fingers
  that report. Seven is fourth, in its own right, with 25.53 held and 0.8 sealed, and goes to
  the National Measure at Saltmere in the third month with Halden License, Old Four and
  Twenty-Eight. Anwen has a summer on the night van. Perrin has his Hall's second team. Kellan
  has a yard. Jude has a yield line. Pru has a sheet and a red tag. Selka Dray will be on her
  feet. Mara has one reading hand on a card until the eleventh month. Orla has a card from the
  south coast.
- Withheld for Book Two and after, intact: what the disk is for; the first book; the cause of
  the Step; why a hold stood; what the notch and the lamps are; who else has dreamed; the
  filter; the Makers; Kiva's classification; whether three doors can be made safe, taught, or
  done twice.

**Material drift, flagged for the editorial pass:** sentence rhythm. The prose is far
more clipped than the formula: mean and median sentence length are roughly half the
target, the short-sentence share is too high, the long-sentence share is about a tenth of
the target, and readability lands near grade 2 instead of 6.8. Paragraph length is close.
Dialogue-tag density is about 2.5× the target. The progression-vocabulary overshoot is
partly intended (front-loaded first sixth of the book) and partly a broad counter. The
focused same-author repair should lengthen and join narrative sentences (especially
interior and expository passages), reduce reporting-verb tags, and lengthen scene
sections, without changing events. Chapter lengths average 5,264 words; at this pace the
book would run about 253,000 words, below `BOOK_MAP.md`'s 270,000–315,000 working range.
The repair pass can recover much of that without padding.

1. Snapshot 4 is dated 2152, just past the plan's ~2135–2150 range.

   (c. 2446–2450). Only the reader can make that link.
3. Tamar Vey is a former Home Line ward-holder (hand door) with a limp.
4. The Kennel bottle-rift's lattice was impressed by an unnamed Glory master about sixty
   years ago.
5. Kiva's left first two fingers are planted with a temporary numbness that leaves them
   "duller" at exit. Permanent loss is still reserved for Movement 5.
6. Rhea Sorn knew Ilse Corran and recognizes the disk, but keeps that note private.

Secondary-viewpoint sections total 5,385 words across six people (about 10.4%); Kiva
holds about 89.6%. That is a plain section word count, not a craft measurement.

### What was written

Chapters 9–13 are the connected spawn-kill preparation: Kiva's first day on the Crooked
Line third team; the Kennel warden's teaching of husk anatomy, the four declared roles,
the abort call, the sixty-count of open ash, printing and dark hands; the catch learned
one-handed with two dull fingers; the Old Yard tin drill and the no-shape drill; Orla's
sponsor's floor and her narrow written certification (recovery only); Team Seven's first
contained floor, printed by Kellan's lit blade and salted; a second floor with a skinned
husk and the team's first correct abort; and the supervised field Handful at a held tear
on the Sag Line under Hollis Vane, passed at 20.8 drams tinned. Chapter 14 grades the
yield (yield, grade, resonance), divides the share, and carries the team's decision.
Chapters 15–16 are the first sanctioned meet, a 9–7 loss to Stonehand Hall's third team,
Noa Bexley's first sanctioned measurements, and Ysra Crest's review.

Held back as the packet requires: the disk is cold throughout; no deliberate two-door
sequence is attempted or completed; the two fingers are still dull and still recovering;
the Unroofed Sky does not recur and is not named; the filter, the disk, the Makers and the
cause of the Fall are not explained. The cohort and the spawn season are shown as two
separate oddities that nobody on the page connects.

### Not done in this run

No editorial review, cold read, repair pass, metric scoring, commit or push. The formula's
sentence, paragraph, readability, progression-vocabulary and development-cadence targets
were written toward but **have not been measured** for these chapters. Nothing here should
be read as a passing score.

During drafting the author corrected continuity slips only, as the task allows: elapsed-day
phrases, two minor-name collisions (a walk-on first called Pryor, too close to Pryce; a
walk-on first called Hallam, too close to Halloran), spelling normalized to Movement One's
usage (color, practice, draft, story, curb), and three plot-logic lines in chapter 10 so
that Anwen's Team Eleven option falls on the same bell that calls Team Seven and Orla's
tin requirement is five nights running.

### Choices the owner should review

1. **Noa Bexley and Hollis Vane are written as men (he/him).** `CHARACTERS.md` gives no
   pronouns for either. This is the author's choice, made because the rest of the
   continuing cast skews heavily female; it is easy to reverse.
2. Hollis Vane's own door is never shown. He runs a four-person Home Line hold and carries
   a slate. He knew Orla Dane as a nineteen-year-old (she took him down his first culvert).
   He does not see or remark on the disk.
3. **School roster versus license team.** Kiva's roster place is the Crooked Line third
   team (captain Jude Talley; Pru Finch; Noa Bexley), which fights school meets. Team
   Seven (Renn, Pryce, Cade, Rowan) continues as a cross-school field-license team that
   holds the yield book. The first-step call order is trial-score order; Team Seven stood
   fortieth of forty.
4. New mechanics put on the page: the four roles (Kill, Hold, Carry, Catch); the abort
   word (*off*, three times); **the count** (fresh ash is open for about sixty counts);
   **dark hands** (nothing lit within three paces during the count; the catcher owns the
   floor and may call *dark*); **printing**; loose ash leaning toward the nearest worked
   pattern; **sweepings** (ash that settles untinned is Grade Third at best and does not
   count); a Handful is 24 drams by the book, a tin holds 6, and the pass mark is 20
   tinned at Grade Second or better with nothing printed, nothing fouled and nobody hurt.
5. Share arithmetic: civic half; casualty reserve a quarter of the remainder; schools half
   of what is left, by head; the team the rest. 20.8 drams gives 10.4 / 2.6 / 3.9 / 3.9.
   Any entry in the team book needs all four signatures.
6. **The ash-share decision:** 0.5 returned to the Wren House reference shelf; 1.0 sealed
   as medical reserve and kept ward-trace; 1.0 for a catch kit of the team's own; 1.4 for
   two Kennel floors. Nothing for a meet reserve, at Kiva's own request, and she nearly
   runs dry in the meet as a result.
7. **The spawn variation** is a "skin": a ward-like sheen over a Handful's front and
   shoulders that turns a lit blade and faces the brightest lit thing. Four on the Kennel
   floor in the month, none in sixty years of Kennel book before; one on the Sag Line; its
   ash reads **W-trace** on the resonance comb, where Handful ash has always read plain.
   Kiva can feel it through the pan without a door ("curved").
8. **The cohort sign:** median Hold 6.1 against forty years at 5.3–5.5; three Fire
   readings at seventeen against two in the previous ten years; three first-years beat a
   31-year-old Stonehand load plate in one afternoon. Perrin was withdrawn from that
   exhibition by his cracked ribs and tells Kiva not to write it in her book.
9. **Noa's finding:** Kiva's draw on the still door alone is about 0.93 on a sanctioned
   board (the bench gave 3.1 with all three doors woken), and a door she has shut cleanly
   recovers its Hold while she works another. Ash is one reserve; the doors tire
   separately. One day's readings; Ysra orders them continued with Kiva's monthly written
   consent. This plants rotation as the long-event advantage without any hand-off.
10. In the meet's third carry, Kiva's braced stance stops Lowen Brask and Jude Talley's
    touch lands in the stop. Orla names it a hand-off **across two people**, explicitly
    not Kiva's and not a sequence. It is intended as the felt reference for Movement
    Three's first true two-door sequence.
11. Mara's review: tuning is suspended, repair and reconciliation continue, hearing on the
    9th before Examiner Rusk. Mast six, which Mara declined to recalibrate, rang four
    minutes before masts five and seven on the 28th; Hollis Vane sent the Works a note
    saying so. Outcome left open.
12. The Flask qualifiers are moved up to the last week of the month after the meet; Team
    Seven stands seventeenth of thirty-one by yield.
13. The month is treated as having thirty days (the 30th is followed by the 1st). It is
    still not named on the page.

### Material choices in the repair the owner should review

1. **Draw is defined as a ratio, lower is better.** Noa says it once in chapter 11: what a
   fighter burned on a piece of work, set against what the book says that work should
   cost. This keeps Movement One's direction (3.1 is bad, 0.82 is good). The brief's
   wording was "usable output relative to the relevant book/bench expectation"; the page
   keeps the ratio-against-expectation sense and does not reverse the direction.
2. **0.93 is kept, with its load correction stated.** Ysra reads "eleven hundredths and a
   hair on the tape, set against the book's twelve" for a four-minute contested hold.
   0.112 / 0.12 = 0.93. The board's 0.87 to 0.98 and Kellan's "eleven hundredths" stand.
3. **"Live-cell events" covers a held rift and the Kennel lattice**, said once by Kellan
   in chapter 9 as the Chair's clerk's reading.
4. **Team Eleven does not get its floor.** Vane shuts the tear after the early husk
   rather than seat a new hand-door in front of it. Had Anwen taken Mace's offer she
   would not have been licensed on the 28th. Nobody on the page says so.
5. **License-day count:** fourteen teams called (thirteen plus Team Nineteen, in the
   place of a team the warden had not passed fit), Team Seven fourteenth, Team Eleven a
   fifteenth waiting behind. Call positions no longer coincide with team numbers:
   Nineteen stood 18th, Eleven 9th; Kiva's log reads T33, T14, T26.
6. **The Home Line drops its wall** before the second husk is cut, so the experts keep
   the dark-hands rule.
7. **Friction added in three existing scenes:** the Kennel benches and floor (chapter 9),
   Selka Dray on the Sag Line (chapter 13), and the Stonehand gallery (chapter 15). None
   is answered or resolved.
8. **One authority stays unpersuaded:** the meet referee signs the card clean and writes
   on its back that a fighter who must be flagged between doors is "supervised," not
   safe. Ysra reads it without remark.
9. **Orla's second condition is shown met:** six seals of six with the bad hand on the
   24th, cited in the certificate.
10. **The escalation pairing** is remarked on once, by Noa. Rhea's card carries the tear
    count alone. Ysra cites both reports as grounds for the Flask change with no comment
    on their relation. "Strong year" appears twice; "dirty spring" once.

## Movement Two targeted recheck — 2026-10-10

- Report: `editor/MOVEMENT-002-TARGETED-RECHECK.md`.
- Reviewer: a separate Claude Opus 5.5 review-only session with the repaired and frozen
  editions, both reviews, the repair brief/report, both continuity checkpoints, and the
  governing canon. It did not write story material.
- Verdict: **PASS** on all three repair priorities. No second prose repair was justified.
- The verifier independently rebuilt the calendar, license-day clock and team count,
  meet score, Draw ratio, ash shares, Hold figures, and key geography. It confirmed the
  added school resistance reads as unresolved social pressure, the adult cadences are
  more distinct, the escalation remains a seed rather than a proof, and Chapter 14 now
  has forward pressure while preserving every protected element.
- Accepted canon: Draw is ash spent against the book expectation (`1.0` standard; lower
  is more efficient); twelve hundredths is the book expectation for the contested

word counts, not craft measurements.

### What was written

Chapters 17–18 open the Flask preparation: Kiva's first deliberate two-door attempts
(Stride into stance, one bound, six misses and an empty ration by Thursday); Warden Druce's
teaching of the Flask as a "string" of eight knots taken as a schedule by a four-team
company; Mara's hearing before Examiner Rusk; the first purchased Kennel floor (the purse
sheet); a brief lost meet at the Wren House; and a professional Graywater response to a
live Flask at the Ferren weir, watched from the bank. Chapters 19–20 are the return meet
against Lowen Brask's Stonehand third team at the Crooked Line's own court (9–8, won), in
which Kiva breaks her word to her sponsor, tries the sequence under home rule nine, and
wrecks her knee; the team's correction; the second Kennel floor; and attempts eleven to
fourteen. Chapters 21–23 are the Flask qualifier at the Pit: the fifth company's decision
to leave no gate lit, Team Seven's clean rung, the string taking the arena's bus, the
professional response, Kiva's first correct deliberate two-door sequence under a Home Line
field direction, the disk warming afterward, and a waking fragment of the dream. Chapter 24
is the easing, the grading, the board's ruling, the share, the investment vote, and the
evidence that opens Movement Four.

Held back as the packet requires: the disk warms once, only after the sequence has
finished, and does nothing else; the sequence is not repeated and is not certified; no
stable two- or three-door configuration is held; no rank changes; the two fingers are
eased back to where they were and are neither cured nor lost; the Unroofed Sky is not
named and supplies no tactic; the filter, the Makers, the disk's origin and the cause of
the Fall are not explained; the W-trace medical reserve is spent only on a healer's easing.

### Not done in this run

No editorial review, cold read, repair pass, metric scoring, frozen edition, commit or
push. The formula's sentence, paragraph, readability, progression-vocabulary and
development-cadence targets were written toward and **have not been measured** for these
chapters. Nothing here should be read as a passing score.

During drafting the author corrected continuity slips only, as the task allows: the
distance to the home line in the meet's first carry (sixteen paces); the score on the
board late in the third carry (eight to five); Jude's rung count in the third company (a
six-knot string); two elapsed-time phrases ("a fortnight"); one line in which Selka Dray
knew of the Rowan household filing (removed, with Kiva's two answering phrases); one
bulk-of-ash image in chapter 17; one phrase in chapter 24 that turned a nosebleed into a
split lip; and the keeper's shelf at the Pit, which first had a
chained gangway plank and now can be reached only through the keeper's door, with the
lever's four-hundredweight requirement stated in the same speech.

### Choices the owner should review

Full detail is in `provenance/MOVEMENT-003-CONTINUITY.md`, section 9. The largest:

1. **The sequence is Stride into stance** (Ember door shut at the top of the bound, a
   doorless fall "as long as it takes to say *two*," the still door set under the landing
   "like a pan"). It opens the Pit's sprung south link, a task that needs a Stride to
   reach and a still-door to hold. It lasts about nine seconds and costs 0.31 of a dram.
2. **It is done outside her certificate**, under a Home Line field direction given one
   minute after the foreman struck the event from the Circuit calendar. Orla refuses to
   certify it, states what she has seen, and takes the consequence on herself. The board
   rules that no sanctioned-event incident occurred. Orla's standard afterward: "Show me
   six."
3. **The new spawn expression:** the skin does not break when the top knot is cut; it
   slides to the next knot, and when the blind string is left with nothing lit it walks to
   the nearest pattern (the arena's bus), lets cords down through the gratings, closes its
   skin all round, stops reading people, and walks the bus toward the Ferren pump bank.
   Opening the link returns it to an ordinary skinned Flask. Rung-one ash reads **W, full;
   I, trace** (the comb's seventh reed, glossed as induction).
4. **The team's mistake is Kiva's own notion** (whole floor dark, taken from the meet's
   lesson), adopted by the company on a show of hands after the foreman said he would
   leave a gate. The board writes a new rule and grants a **"second step, attached."**
5. **Selka Dray**, who objected to Kiva's seat and has not changed her mind, is the
   second hand at the lever and keeps Kiva's count. **Lowen Brask** commands the Kiln's
   lid crew and uses the Crooked Line's count-in to bring it down on a second string.
6. **Three evidence hooks for Movement Four:** a T-0 entry in the Pit's branch book (every
   lamp on an isolated ring, once); a hand-copied 2446 register entry with a margin note in
   a retired record hand; and a Home Line bearer's book and reasons ledger that Orla has
   asked Hollis Vane to open. None is explained.
7. **Chapter 22 is short** (3,911 words) because the response continues without a break
   into chapter 23. The chapter boundary could move in review without rewriting.

### Material choices in the repair the owner should review

Full detail, with before-and-after text, is in `editor/MOVEMENT-003-REPAIR-REPORT.md`.

1. **Healer Aske is "he"** (three words in chapter 24). The brief said to give a pronoun
   where the prose was avoiding one and did not say which. Easy to reverse.
2. **Three minor names changed:** Tolliver to **Gower**, Merrin to **Edda**, Warden Tilling
   to **Sedge**. Searched against the registry and canon; none matched.
3. **The race is raised on the terrace and honestly dismissed** by Bettan and Vane ("There's
   no pattern in a bar"). Kiva reads her own weir note against herself and is glad to have
   it answered. The company slate records the objection and the answer.
4. **Kiva's mark is set clear of gratings and benches**, not of "the board's feeder," so she
   no longer avoids copper in one scene and dismisses it in the next.
5. **One new observation on the Monday:** Kiva thinks she sees a heap lift toward a lit
   gate, and one such team comes out at forty-nine. She does not know that the gate cost
   the dram and writes no reason. This gives the dark floor a second honest motive; the
   owner may prefer it cut.
6. **Vane owns the link-before-lid trade** aloud and on the slate ("Pumps before doorway").
   The board's findings were left word for word and do not mention it.
7. **Costs now paid on the page:** Anwen's list and an exchange that is left unsettled;
   Gower at the table; Rhea owning the ward slide and going to Stroud; Stroud declining to
   say what she thinks of the dark floor; a catcher from Team Twenty-Eight at the
   standings; Perrin's answer to Vane.
8. **The infirmary healer's account is paid** (by Orla) and the knee's ban becomes Aske's
   fortnight, to the 13th. Kiva carries the crate.
9. **Removed from this movement:** Orla's remark about the superseded stacks, and the
   teaser about Rhea's two-word card. Both remain in the frozen edition if wanted back.
10. **Noa no longer knows of Kiva's register entries.** He has the keeper's remark and
    writes "Find out what a T-0 is." Noa stays he/him; the brief's "her" for Noa was read
    as a slip.
11. **Kiva tells Mara she will take the 2446 margin to the Library's reader herself.** A
    small decision of her own, in place of Orla's echo. It points at Movement Four without
    binding it.
12. **The Halden man reads the tape "on the first of next month."** That is thirty days
    from Friday the 1st on a thirty-day month, the only month length the book has stated.
13. **The countdown to the link now closes:** 22 and 16 on the hobble, 8 at the lip, and
    the cords at the head of the ramp as her count reaches one.
14. **Chapter 24 is 8,571 words** (was 6,319), the longest chapter in the book so far.
15. **Sixty-three narration joins outside chapter 24**, in quiet passages of chapters 17
    (14), 19 (17), 20 (14) and 21 (18), made under the brief's owner ruling on narration.
    No fact changes in them.

Secondary-viewpoint sections total 5,811 words across five people (Rhea 1,650, Noa 1,618,
Vane 1,021, Orla 766, Anwen 756), about 12.9%; Kiva holds about 87.1%. The eight chapters
have 41 sections (about 1,100 words a section). These are plain section word counts, not
craft measurements. Hollis Vane is a viewpoint for the first time.

### What was written

Chapters 25–26 turn the fortnight's stand-down into four separate searches. Kiva takes the
2446 margin to the Compact Library's reader, learns that the Even Hand asked about the lamps
that night, is shown what a disputed box costs to open, and finds her sponsor's name in the
request book sixteen years back. Noa learns what a T-0 is and that Kiva already had four,
finds a notch on all sixteen pens of the Pit's tape, and reads the Halden patent behind the
comb's seventh reed. Orla owns having cut a report to fit. Kiva puts the papers in front of
her team on Anwen's last night, and Anwen leaves unreconciled by her own choice. Chapter 27
has a Home Line ledger entry of 2471 read (a two-door woman, a "crossing," and a hand that
did not come back), the request made with Mara's hand, the travelling mast, Orla's six rules,
and the first sanctioned go: a called abort. Chapter 28 is the tries: one crossing on the
cross, one "stack" that lands softly at a third of the cost and is called a miss by Noa
against his own wishes, and a second crossing that leaves two fingers white for forty-one
counts. Chapters 29–30 are the Graywater shadow shift and the Weighbridge Street call: a
qualifier's habits fail when a student lure is read by a Flask; a professional is pinned;
shed workers lift on Perrin's count; roles and reasons are declared again; a first-year
walks a Flask down a street as a declared lamp; Team Seven takes a second husk the corrected
way; Kiva catches three rungs behind Pike. Chapter 31 is the reconciliation on the wharf (a
stepped bag withheld from a tenement on a catcher's word, a comb and a warden's four-century
rule), Rhea Sorn's slate and her one piece of guidance, and Kiva's reading of Hesper Annick's
fever entry under two observers. Chapter 32 is the claims on the bag, Kellan's choice, and
the bearer's book at the Home Line hall, in which Kiva reads Ilse Corran's reason and writes
the disk's one warmth into a column that has said "nothing" for five hundred years.

Held back as the packet requires: the disk stays cold through two sanctioned crossings and
the call, and neither it nor the dream supplies a tactic; no fever and no recurrence; the
dream is confirmed as shared imagery only, with no date, cause, meaning or use; the disk's
origin is a lost first book and "We do not know what it is for"; Kiva opens no door in the
field and stays inside her certificate; the tries stand at two of six; no rank changes; the
fingers are neither cured nor lost; the sealed W reserve is untouched; no new spawn expression
is introduced; Anwen is not on Team Seven after the 10th; the 10:24 pattern is measured more
closely and not explained.

### Not done in this run

No editorial review, cold read, repair pass, metric scoring, frozen edition or narration, and
no commit, push or publication by the author (see "Branch" above for what another process
did). The formula's sentence, paragraph, readability,
progression-vocabulary and development-cadence targets were written toward and **have not
been measured** for these chapters. Nothing here should be read as a passing score.

During drafting the author corrected continuity slips only, as the task allows, by small
in-place edits made directly after each chapter was written: elapsed-time phrases (Aske's
week; "a week after" the Pit; Mara's and Kiva's acquaintance with Orla; Noa's "Saturday";
"last Thursday"); what Orla's suspension covered and why she took Kiva on; who was in the Low
Lane on the 4th; that Mara's sealed record is one page, of the 29th; the count of earlier
jams and goes; the number of people in the yard; a Kennel-floor comparison; the third team's
record with Kiva; that Pru Finch is not a Wren House student; the bell codes; where Anwen's
line was working on the 23rd; the tear's times (2:14, 2:24, about 2:32); street geometry (the
mark fourteen paces above the tear, the lure two paces below the hem, the gate eight paces up,
the Pellow a dozen paces below); how long Agnes Roake waited for her pump; and a third
"nineteen years" that became twenty. Two changes were larger. **The Step:** the first wording
made every bank in Meridian a stepped bank, which contradicted the pump bank going "into
step" as an alarm in chapter 24; it now belongs to Halden's yards, refineries, lift yards and
big tram sheds, and municipal banks are unstepped. **The fifth husk:** the section in which
Team Seven declares its corrected plan was rewritten once so that the lure stands within
twelve paces of the husk and the cloth above the Pellow, and so that the officer's correction
is "who's watching the gap." Four short passages were added to chapter 29 after its first
write because the scene wanted them (Pike's cord grip; the two residents who cannot be moved;
Pike looking all the way round before he kneels; Anwen's earned wall). None of this was a
review or a scored pass.

### Choices the owner should review

Full detail is in `provenance/MOVEMENT-004-CONTINUITY.md`, section 10. The largest:

1. **The district is Weighbridge Street and the Pellow Building** from prologue snapshot 8.
   The building's cell book still carries the 2247 installer's reason on its first leaf and
   has lost his name; its warden applies it to the call's ash.
2. **The students' failure is their own first-step method.** A lure for a husk is a lamp for
   a Flask. Kiva gives the word, a Graywater blade's leg is broken, and Rhea Sorn takes the
   remark as the shift's. Team Seven stays attached.
3. **"Stepped" ash and the Halden patent** ("however produced"). The comb's seventh reed is
   tied to a foundation patent; the Flask's bottom two knots read I, full; the bag is withheld
   from the tenement and is now under three claims with a hearing on the 3rd. This is the
   movement's largest invention and the main engine handed to Movement Five.
4. **"A crossing"** is the Home Line's older word for a sequence, from a 2471 field direction
   to a two-door woman, Tansy Wold, whose left hand did not return. She is not a bearer.
5. **A "stack"** is a third kind of go: soft, a third of the cost, wrong, and nearly signed
   by Orla. Noa corrects both of them from the tape.
6. **Two of six**, with exactly enough ash for four more and a new healer's price of one go a
   week. The fingers stay away longer each time.
7. **Kellan accepts the Halden license team for the Regional** and stays on Team Seven's book.
8. **Rhea Sorn speaks to Kiva**, gives one bounded piece of guidance, tells her about Ilse
   Corran and the culvert, and says she will burn a card without saying what was on it.
9. **The bearer's book** in full, including a recall clause, Ilse's reason ("Will they put
   why?"), the Line's record that it overrode Mara in 2669, and **Kiva's own entry.**
10. **The reading** is done under two silent observers; Kiva counts through it, all three
    doors stir, and the sister of the Even Hand notices and has it recorded.
11. **Orla read the Annick notebook in 2664 and withheld it**; she owns that and refuses to
    tell Kiva what it says. Mara chooses not to read it and has her own page sealed and timed.
12. **Movement length is 45,124 words**, at the foot of the working range, with no chapter
    over 6,500.
